Let me think about the proof.

We have STLC CBV with locally nameless representation. Need strong normalization.

Approach: Logical relation with unary type-indexed predicate. Since we use locally nameless, we can define the logical relation on terms, parameterized by an environment for free variables (i.e., interpretation of the context).

Standard approach (like Software Foundations Stlc / "Strong normalization" chapter):

Define:
- `sn t := strongly_normalizing t` (already given, but with inductive def).
- Logical relation for values maybe:
  - `R Bool v := value v /\ ...`? Actually in CBV, we typically define the logical relation on *terms*: `LR T t := strongly_normalizing t /\ (value t -> ...)`? Hmm.

Let's think. Classic CBV SN proof: Define `SN` as strong normalization. Define `R T t` for closed-ish terms:
- `R Bool t := SN t` (or `SN t /\ (value t -> ... )`).
- `R (T1 -> T2) t := SN t /\ forall u, value u -> R T1 u -> R T2 (app t u)`. Actually for CBV one typically needs `R (T1->T2) t := SN t /\ forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (t u)` but with `u` not necessarily value. Hmm, but application of a non-value is also fine, since the function steps. Let's think carefully.

Actually, the classic Girard-Tait for CBV: define the logical predicate on values:
- `R Bool v := True` for values? But then to prove SN of terms we need a "term interpretation" `[T] t` = SN t and if t reduces to value v then v in value relation.
  
Common formulation: Define `C T t := SN t /\ (forall t', t -->* t' -> value t' -> V T t')` where V is the value relation. Then:
- `V Bool v := True`.
- `V (T1 -> T2) v := forall u, C T1 u -> C T2 (app v u)`.

But we need to handle open terms / free variables via context interpretation. Standard locally nameless approach: use "the term context interpretation": environment maps each atom to a term u, and Gamma is a context; `R Gamma t := ...`? Actually for SN, since free variables might be untyped? We need closed terms at the end (empty context), but proving the fundamental theorem requires induction over typing derivations where context is arbitrary. So we need an interpretation of contexts as substitutions, and show that typing is preserved.

Alternative approach avoiding explicit environment machinery: use the "semantic typing" with free variable assignment as a function `env : atom -> tm` and a context `Gamma`, with `env_ok Gamma env := forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> C T (env x)`. Then prove `has_type Gamma t T -> forall env, env_ok Gamma env -> C T (t substituted with env)`.

But we need substitution. Locally nameless: free variables are atoms; substitution of free variables is straightforward: `subst x u t` replaces `tm_fvar x` with u, respecting binders (bvar not affected; abs doesn't bind fvar, since locally nameless uses bvar under binders). Actually with locally nameless, free variable substitution is trivial and does not require avoiding capture, because bound variables are bvars and free ones are fvars; substituting a term for fvar x can capture? Inside `tm_abs T t`, there could be `tm_bvar` indices, and u might have bvars... Actually free variable substitution: substitute u for fvar x in t. Under abstractions, no shifting needed since binders are nameless (de Bruijn) and u inserted as-is? Hmm, wait: In locally nameless, when a term is under a binder, free variables are still fvars; substitution of a fvar with a term that has free bvars could be captured? Free bvars don't occur in locally closed terms, but intermediate terms might have bvars (e.g., t in tm_abs T t has bvar 0). Substituting u under the binder: u's bvar 0 would refer to the outer binder mistakenly. This is the classic issue; but if u is locally closed, no problem. In our semantic proof, environment terms are closed? Hmm, not necessarily: In the fundamental theorem for open terms, the environment maps fvars to terms that may themselves have free fvars? But since we only need closed terms at the end, maybe we can restrict to "all fvars in context map to locally closed terms". Alternatively, define environment as mapping atoms to terms and prove semantics for open terms via a different route.

Alternative route avoiding substitution into arbitrary terms: Interpret contexts as *lists of values*? Or use simultaneous substitution with proper shifting.

Alternative: Use the standard locally nameless "open" operation. In the fundamental theorem, T_Abs case: Gamma |-- lambda : T1, t, with body t possibly containing bvar 0 and fvars. To show C (T1->T2) (abs T1 t), we need for all u with C T1 u, C T2 (app (abs T1 t) u). Now app (abs T1 t) u steps to `open t u` — that's the substitution of u for bvar 0 in t, not fvar substitution. So actually we need to reason about `open t u`, where u is arbitrary (not necessarily closed). Hmm, but in the semantic proof, we might show C T2 (open t u) using the semantic interpretation of the body under an environment mapping the bound variable... This is the standard "CR" argument requiring reasoning about free variables.

A neat approach: Define logical relation directly on locally nameless terms with a context Γ, using a *valuation* assigning to each free atom a term, and interpret bvars via a list of terms too? That gets complicated.

Let's define:
```
Fixpoint subst (σ : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => σ x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst σ t1) (subst σ t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst σ t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst σ t1) (subst σ t2) (subst σ t3)
  end.
```

The classic resolution: define R_T = {t : SN t and all value reducts in V_T}, and prove that application of reducible terms is reducible via a lemma ("R is closed under application": if R_{T1->T2} t1 and R_{T1} t2 then R_{T2} (app t1 t2)). Let me reconsider. Standard reducibility candidate theory handles this via the "CR3" property: a term is reducible if all its one-step reducts are reducible, *provided the term is neutral*? In lambda calculus, a "neutral" term is one that is not an abstraction; and CR3 says if all reducts of a neutral term are reducible then the term is reducible. For CBV, "neutral" terms: variables, applications, if, and also true/false? In CBV, true/false are values but applying them gets stuck; they're not neutral in the same sense but we can treat them as neutral-ish because applications of a non-abstraction value are stuck. The key property: if t is not an abstraction, then every reduct of app t u is either app t' u with t --> t', or app t u' with t value and u --> u'. So it's determined by reducts of t and u. And if t is not abstraction, app t u is also not an abstraction. So we can define C T t for non-abstractions via: all one-step reducts are C, and then closure... 

Actually here's a cleaner way: define C T t *only* as:
- C T t := SN t /\ (forall t', t -->* t' -> value t' -> V T t')
where V is the value relation:
- V Bool v := True
- V (T1→T2) v := forall u, C T1 u -> C T2 (app v u)   [or with value restriction + C]
This is the standard "reducibility" definition. Let's explore whether the fundamental theorem is easier.

Properties:
- C T t → SN t.
- C T t → t -->* t' → C T t' (closure under multi-step, since SN and value-reducts).
- For a value v: V T v → C T v? Need SN v. Is every value SN? true/false yes; abs: SN trivial (no steps). So values are SN. So C T v iff V T v (given SN v).
- Fundamental theorem: Γ ⊢ t:T, γ maps vars to values with V_{Γ(x)}(γx) [or C], then C T (γt).
  - T_Var: γx is C (as value) by the relation.
  - T_App: IH: C T1→T2 (γt1), C T1 (γt2). Need C T2 (app γt1 γt2). By definition: SN(app ...) and for all value reduct w of app..., V T2 w. 
    - SN: Take any multi-step app γt1 γt2 -->* w with value w. Need V T2 w. Hmm, analyzing the multi-step requires a lemma that C is closed under application (the classic result). 
    This is the "CR application" lemma: C (T1→T2) t1 -> C T1 t2 -> C T2 (app t1 t2). Proof: SN app t1 t2: all reducts SN? If t1 --> t1': app t1 t2 --> app t1' t2; by IH on SN t1? Let me think about the cleanest formalization for THIS specific language, exploiting structural properties.

Observation: The language is STLC with CBV. A well-known relatively simple SN proof for STLC uses a *logical predicate on terms* defined by well-founded recursion on the type, with the key lemma "if t is SN and all its one-step reducts are in C T, then t ∈ C T", plus a separate handling for abstractions. But the abstraction case needs typing of the body.

Let me formalize the TAPL-style reducibility for CBV carefully and check each lemma. Actually, let's reconsider Option A with arrow closure over ALL C-arguments (not just values), because T_App becomes trivial. The only hard part is T_Abs closure. Let's fully work it out.

Definition (recursion on ty):
```
Fixpoint C (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  lc t /\
  SN t /\
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => True
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => forall u, C T1 u -> C T2 (tm_app t u)
  end.
```

Fundamental theorem T_Abs:
Given H : forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Γ x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2.
Need C (T1→T2) (tm_abs T1 (subst σ t1)) where σ ≡ env_ok Γ.

Components:
(a) lc (tm_abs T1 (subst σ t1)): need lc_at 1 (subst σ t1). We need a lemma linking typing to lc of the opened body. Since `open t1 (fvar x)` is typable, and all typable terms are lc, we get lc (open t1 (fvar x)). Then need lc_at 1 (subst σ t1) from lc (open t1 (fvar x)), x fresh, σ images lc. Prove lemma: `lc_subst` : (forall y, lc (σ y)) -> lc_at k (subst σ t) <-> ??? with t's bvars... Actually subst doesn't change bvars, so lc_at k (subst σ t) iff lc_at k t and all σ images lc_at k? Hmm, but σ images may contain bvars! Under binders, substituting a closed image... if lc (σ y) = lc_at 0, then lc_at k (σ y) for any k? lc_at k means bvars < k; if lc_at 0 means no bvars, then lc_at k holds for any k (we can prove lc_at_mono? lc_at k t -> lc_at k' t for k ≤ k'? Yes bvars < k ≤ k'). So `lc_subst`: if lc (σ y) for all y, then lc_at k (subst σ t) ↔ lc_at k t. Because subst replaces fvars with closed terms (which satisfy lc_at k for any k), and preserves bvars. Straightforward induction.
Then lc_at 1 (subst σ t1) follows from lc_at 1 t1. And lc_at 1 t1 from lc (open t1 (fvar x))? We need the inverse of opening: `lc_at_1_of_lc_open`: lc (open t (fvar x)) -> lc_at 1 t. Prove by induction on t:
  - bvar i: open (bvar 0) = fvar x if i=0 (well, if eqb 0 i); else bvar i. lc (result): if i=0 → fvar x lc; if i>0 → bvar i lc requires i < 0, impossible. So lc_at 1 (bvar i): need i < 1, i.e., i=0 or i<1; for i=0 ok; for i>0, hypothesis gives lc_at 0 (bvar i) → i<0 contradiction. Good.
  - fvar: trivial.
  - app: induction.
  - abs T t: open (abs T t) = abs T (open_rec 1 (fvar x) t); lc means lc_at 1 (open_rec 1 (fvar x) t). Need lc_at 1 of abs = lc_at 2 t. So need: lc_at 1 (open_rec 1 (fvar x) t) -> lc_at 2 t.     - bvar i: open_rec k u (bvar i) = u if i=k else bvar i. If i=k: need i < S k, true. If i≠k: lc_at k (bvar i) gives i<k<Sk.
    - abs: IH with k+1 gives lc_at (S(S k)) body from lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) u body). Need lc_at (S k) (abs T body) = lc_at (S(S k)) body. ✓.
    - app/if/true/false easy.
  Great.

Let's step back and adopt the standard *reducibility candidates* framework which is designed to avoid these ad hoc arguments. The standard framework:

For CBV, define two predicates by recursion on type T:
- `V T : tm -> Prop` (values):
  - V Bool v := True.
  - V (T1→T2) v := forall u, C T1 u -> C T2 (app v u).  (for all terms? or values)
- `C T : tm -> Prop` (terms):
  - C T t := SN t /\ (forall t', t -->* t' -> value t' -> V T t').

This is a well-founded? Mutual recursion between V at T1/T2 and C at smaller types, so a single Fixpoint on T can define both (C uses V at same T; V at arrow uses C at T1, T2 smaller; C at T uses V at T; so define a pair via Fixpoint: 
```
Fixpoint C T t := SN t /\ (forall t', multi step t t' -> value t' -> V T t')
with V T v := match T with Bool => True | Arrow T1 T2 => forall u, C T1 u -> C T2 (app v u) end.
```
Coq mutual Fixpoint on ty: C calls V at same T; V calls C at T1 and T2. For the mutual fixpoint, recursive calls must be on structurally smaller arguments? In Coq's guard checker, mutual recursion allows calls to the other function on the same or smaller? Actually the guard condition for mutual fixpoints: a recursive call in the body of C to V with the same argument T is fine? The "principal argument" decreases... For mutual fixpoints, each function has its own principal argument; calls from C to V with T not smaller might fail guard. Hmm. We can instead define a single Fixpoint returning a pair of predicates:
```
Fixpoint CV (T : ty) : (tm -> Prop) * (tm -> Prop) := ...
```
But then using it is awkward (projections). Or define C via V via nested fixpoints: since C at T mentions V at T, and V at T mentions C at T1/T2 (smaller), define Fixpoint C T t := ... and inside, define V by matching on T; but V at arrow needs C at T1 — recursive call on smaller T. Coq might accept a single Fixpoint:

```
Fixpoint C (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  SN t /\
  match T with
  | Bool => True
  | Arrow T1 T2 => forall u, C T1 u -> C T2 (app t u)
  end.
```
This is Option A exactly (without lc), where C Arrow's closure is over all C arguments. The value relation isn't needed.

Alternatively define CV pair with V Bool=True. Hmm.

Let's think about the TAPL-style proof and its key lemmas; maybe it's cleaner in Coq because the "value" restriction aligns with CBV.

Actually, here's a thought: maybe use Option A (closure over all C args) plus the following two key lemmas:

Lemma L1 (C_step): forall T t t', C T t -> t --> t' -> C T t'.
Proof by induction on T.
- Bool: C = lc /\ SN. lc preserved by step (needs lemma `lc_step`), SN preserved. 
- Arrow: C t = lc /\ SN /\ closure. For reduct t': lc, SN. Closure: forall u, C T1 u -> C T2 (app t' u). We have C T2 (app t u) and app t u --> app t' u via ST_App1 needing lc u (C T1 u gives lc). Apply IH at T2 (smaller) to get C T2 (app t' u). ✓.
So L1 requires C includes lc. Also `lc_step` and `sn_step`.

Lemma L2 (C_abs_intro): For all T1 T2 b, lc (abs T1 b) -> (forall u, value u -> C T1 u -> C T2 (open b u)) -> C (T1→T2) (abs T1 b).
Components: lc given. SN (abs) trivial. Closure: forall u, C T1 u -> C T2 (app (abs) u) [note: closure over all C args, not just values].
Proof: by SN-induction on u (C T1 u → SN u).
 Let P u := C T1 u -> C T2 (app (abs T1 b) u).
 Step: given C T1 u. Need C T2 (app abs u).
   Show by the "application-intro" lemma? The reducts:
     - ST_AppAbs (value u, lc abs): z = open b u. Have C T2 z by hypothesis H (requires value u).
     - ST_App2 (value abs, u --> u'): z = app abs u'. C T1 u' by L1, then IH on u' gives C T2 z.
     - ST_App1 (abs --> t1'): impossible.
   So all reducts of `app abs u` are C T2. Additionally, lc (app abs u) and SN (app abs u) [SN from reducts]. But we need the closure property for C T2 (app abs u). Can we get it from a general lemma "term of application shape with all reducts C is C"?

Let's try to prove this general lemma by induction on the type T (not on the term), with an auxiliary well-founded induction on something.

General Lemma (AppIntro): For all T, x, y: if
  (i) lc (app x y),
  (ii) forall z, app x y --> z -> C T z,
  (iii) x is not an abstraction? 
then C T (app x y).

Hmm, but what conditions exactly? Let's attempt a proof by induction on T, and within the Arrow case, we need closure under further application, which requires handling `app (app x y) w`. Its reducts:
  - ST_App1: app x y --> z, then app z w. We have C T z, and C T z's closure gives C T2 (app z w). ✓
  - ST_App2: requires value (app x y) — impossible since app is not a value.
So all reducts of `app (app x y) w` are C T2. Then to apply the induction hypothesis at type T2 (smaller), we need conditions (i)-(iii) for the term app (app x y) w. But its "head" is app x y, which is not an abstraction ✓ (an application can't be an abstraction; syntactically). lc: from lc (app x y) and lc w (C T1 w gives lc) ✓. Condition (ii): all reducts C T2 — shown above using closure of C T z. But wait, condition (ii) is about one-step reducts; we used C T z where z is a reduct of app x y (from ST_App1 case). Fine.
  Hmm, but the induction hypothesis at T2 needs the same shape conditions for term `app (app x y) w`, including condition (ii) "forall z', app (app x y) w --> z' -> C T2 z'". We showed all such z' are C T2 using: ST_App1 case needs C T z for z reduct of app x y, which is exactly condition (ii) of the original. So the proof of condition (ii) for the new term uses only condition (ii) of the original at type T. 

  But we also need to show C T (app x y) itself satisfies lc and SN: lc given; SN from SN_intro + reducts C (condition ii) → each reduct SN. ✓.

  And in the Arrow case, the closure property is: forall w, C T1 w -> C T2 (app (app x y) w). This is what the IH at T2 gives, provided the shape conditions hold for app (app x y) w. ✓.
  
  Base case T = Bool: C Bool t = lc t /\ SN t; have both.

So the AppIntro lemma seems provable by induction on T, but we need to state condition (ii) carefully and prove the induction cleanly. Let's define:

```
Lemma C_app_intro : forall T x y,
  lc (tm_app x y) ->
  (forall z, tm_app x y --> z -> C T z) ->
  C T (tm_app x y).
```
Wait, do we need "x not an abstraction"? In the Arrow closure step, we consider app (app x y) w. For ST_App2 we claimed value (app x y) impossible — true. For ST_AppAbs, we need app x y to be an abstraction — impossible. So the shape "app x y" is crucial; the head is an application. In the inductive step at type T2, the term is `app (app x y) w` which is again of the shape app _ _, so the induction hypothesis applies. Good. We do NOT need any non-abstraction condition beyond the syntactic shape, because the term is an application.

Actually more generally: `C_app_intro T x y` where the term is app x y. In the induction on T, for T = Arrow T1 T2, we prove closure: forall w, C T1 w -> C T2 (app (app x y) w). Apply IH at T2 to the term app (app x y) w:
 - lc: lc (app (app x y) w) from lc (app x y) and lc w (C T1 w → lc).
 - reducts: z with app (app x y) w --> z.
   * ST_App1: app x y --> z0 and z = app z0 w. Condition (ii) of the original gives C T z0. Then C T z0's closure gives C T2 (app z0 w). ✓
   * ST_App2: value (app x y) — contradiction: prove `value_app_absurd : value (tm_app x y) -> False` by inversion.
   * ST_AppAbs: app x y = tm_abs _ _ — contradiction: no constructor of tm makes app equal abs; discriminate.
 So IH at T2 applies. ✓
 
 Great, this lemma is clean! It gives us C for any application-shaped term whose one-step reducts are all C.

T_True/T_False: C Bool true: lc true, SN true, True. Need `sn_true`, `sn_false`: no steps from true/false. Prove with SN_intro + inversion on step.

T_If: Γ ⊢ if t1 t2 t3 : T with t1 : Bool, t2,t3 : T. IH: C Bool (subst σ t1) (which is lc and SN), C T (subst σ t2), C T (subst σ t3). Need C T (if t1' t2' t3').
   Components: lc (if ...): from lc of parts. SN (if ...): all reducts:
     - ST_IfTrue: result t2' (lc t2' from C). Need C T t2'? Already have.
     - ST_IfFalse: result t3', have C.
     - ST_If: t1' --> t1'', result if t1'' t2' t3'. Need C T (if t1'' t2' t3'). Hmm, recursive. We can prove SN by induction on SN t1' (the scrutinee): since C Bool t1' = lc + SN. Let's do SN-induction on t1', with motive P t1' := C Bool t1' -> C T (if t1' t2' t3') (with t2', t3' fixed with C T). 
       Case: given C Bool t1'. Need C T (if t1' t2' t3'). Use a lemma analogous to C_app_intro for `if`: "if-shaped terms": if lc and all reducts C then C. Because if-shaped terms are not abstractions and not applications; their closure? Wait C T (if ...) at arrow type requires closure under application: forall u C T1 u, C T2 (app (if ...) u). The term app (if ...) u is an application; its reducts: ST_App1 when if... steps to z, then app z u; ST_AppAbs? no; ST_App2? value (if ...)? if is not a value, so no. So we can use the general "app-shaped intro" lemma! Because app (if...) u is app-shaped, lc and all reducts C T2:
          - ST_App1: if... --> z0; we have C T z0 (condition ii), then closure gives C T2 (app z0 u).
       So the if case at arrow type can be proven by C_app_intro on the term app (if...) u.
       
       This suggests a general lemma: `C_intro_nonvalue`? Let's define a general lemma for terms t that are "not values and not abstractions"? Hmm. Actually maybe we can prove a uniform lemma:

       Lemma `C_if_intro : forall T b1 b2 b3, lc (tm_if b1 b2 b3) -> (forall z, tm_if b1 b2 b3 --> z -> C T z) -> C T (tm_if b1 b2 b3)`. Proof by induction on T:
         - Bool: lc & SN from reducts.
         - Arrow T1 T2: closure: forall u C T1 u, C T2 (app (if...) u). Use C_app_intro at T2 on x := if..., y := u:
             - lc: lc if and lc u.
             - reducts of app (if...) u:
               ST_App1: if --> z0, C T z0 from hypothesis; closure of C T z0 gives C T2 (app z0 u). ✓
               ST_App2: value (if...) impossible.
               ST_AppAbs: if... = abs impossible.
             So the proof is by `apply C_app_intro` with the same hypothesis; no induction on T needed actually! Wait, C_app_intro itself is proven by induction on T. And here we apply C_app_intro at T2, which is a subterm of T... but we're inside proving C_if_intro by induction on T. Hmm, C_app_intro is already proven globally by induction on its type argument, so it's available for any T2. Great, no issue.
         Actually then C_if_intro is straightforward given C_app_intro, for all T, without induction: 
           - C T (if...) = lc + SN + closure. For closure, apply C_app_intro at T2 (T = T1→T2) to term app (if...) u: need lc and reducts. ✓. And SN: from reducts using SN_intro. lc: given. So no induction on T needed.

Let's define a general "C_intro" lemma for terms with a *head* that is not an abstraction, with all one-step reducts C:
   `C_intro_neutral : forall T t, lc t -> (forall t', t --> t' -> C T t') -> (forall u, ...)`? 

Let's look at what we actually need:
1. For `app x y`: C_app_intro (proven by induction on T) using shape.
2. For `if b1 b2 b3`: prove directly using C_app_intro in the arrow case, as above.
3. For `abs T b`: `C_abs_intro` using semantics of body at values.

Actually, maybe there's an even more general lemma: If t is not an abstraction and all its one-step reducts are C T, then C T t. Is this true? Let's test with t = true (not an abstraction, value, no reducts): C T true would require for arrow T: forall u C T1 u, C T2 (app true u). Is C T2 (app true u) true? app true u: reducts: if u steps, app true u'; if u stuck, no reduct. Does C T2 hold? It's an application-shaped term with all reducts C if we can show... This is essentially "stuck terms are C". Let's see if we can prove the general lemma by induction on T and using C_app_intro for app-shaped subterms:
   - T = Bool: lc & SN.
   - T = Arrow T1 T2: closure: forall u C T1 u, C T2 (app t u). The term app t u is app-shaped. Apply C_app_intro at T2:
       - lc: lc t, lc u.
       - reducts of app t u:
          * ST_App1: t --> t0, C T t0 from hypothesis; closure gives C T2 (app t0 u). ✓
          * ST_App2: value t, u --> u', result app t u'. Need C T2 (app t u'). We have C T1 u' by C_step. But we can't apply closure of C T t (not yet established). However, app t u' is app-shaped; apply C_app_intro again? That would be circular (same term with smaller u? u' is a reduct of u, SN u' smaller in SN sense). We could do an inner SN-induction on u. Hmm.
          * ST_AppAbs: t = abs — excluded by hypothesis "not abstraction".
       So for the ST_App2 case, we need induction on SN u. But we also need condition: value t means t is true or false or abs; not abs → true/false. So t is true/false. Then app t u' has head t (not abstraction!), and its reducts are only ST_App2 (since t is a value that doesn't step). So SN-induction on u works: for u' with u --> u', C T1 u', IH gives... wait IH gives C T2 (app t u')? The motive is P u := C T1 u -> C T2 (app t u) for the *specific* t (true/false). Then yes, IH on u' gives C T2 (app t u') after C_step. But in the ST_App1 case, we need C T2 (app t0 u) where t0 is a reduct of t, which may be an abstraction (e.g., t = if true (abs) x --> abs). We have C T t0 from the hypothesis (all reducts C), and its closure gives C T2 (app t0 u) directly. Fine.
       
       So a general lemma `C_intro_notabs` needs an inner SN-induction on u for the ST_App2 case, with t fixed as a non-abstraction (but during induction, t stays fixed; the reducts of t are handled by the outer hypothesis). Let's formulate:

       Lemma C_intro_notabs : forall T t,
         ~ is_abs t ->
         lc t ->
         (forall t', t --> t' -> C T t') ->
         C T t.
       
       Proof by induction on T.
       - Bool: SN from SN_intro with reducts C; lc given.
       - Arrow T1 T2: we need forall u, C T1 u -> C T2 (app t u).
         Prove by induction on SN u (the argument): motive P u := C T1 u -> C T2 (app t u).
         intro Cu. Apply C_app_intro at T2 to term app t u:
           * lc: lc t, lc u.
           * reducts z of app t u:
             + ST_App1: t --> t0, z = app t0 u. Have C T t0 by outer hypothesis; closure → C T2 (app t0 u). ✓ (no SN-IH needed)
             + ST_App2: value t, u --> u0, z = app t u0. Have C T1 u0 by C_step Cu. Apply SN-IH to u0 with step u-->u0 and C T1 u0. ✓
             + ST_AppAbs: t = abs, contradicts ~ is_abs t. ✓
         Wait: C_app_intro gives C T2 (app t u) provided lc and all reducts C T2. We don't need the outer lc t for C_app_intro's lc condition? Yes: lc (app t u) needs lc t and lc u; lc u from Cu. And lc t is given.
         So the inner SN-induction proves the closure. 
       Great, and no induction on SN t needed because reducts t0 get C from hypothesis, and ST_App2 doesn't change t.
       
       Hmm, but in the ST_App2 case, `C T1 u0` from `C_step` requires the lemma C_step already proven (by induction on T). Fine, C_step is proven before C_intro_notabs. But note: C_step at type T1 uses induction on T1. No circularity (we're proving C_intro_notabs by induction on T; C_step is a separate earlier lemma).
       
       Also, the inner SN-induction: `induction Hsn` where Hsn : SN u. The motive P u := C T1 u -> C T2 (app t u). But the IH will be `forall u', u --> u' -> C T1 u' -> C T2 (app t u')`. Good.

       Hmm wait, there's still an issue: in the ST_App1 case, t --> t0 and C T t0 includes closure over all arguments u (C T1 u). We have Cu. So C T2 (app t0 u). ✓. 

       And lc t requirement: is it needed? For C_app_intro's lc premise yes. Actually C_app_intro requires lc (app t u); lc u from Cu; lc t from given. ✓.

   So the general lemma works for non-abstractions. Note `~ is_abs t` can be stated as `forall T b, t <> tm_abs T b` or an inductive predicate. We can just use `(forall T b, t <> tm_abs T b)`.

   Then for the abstraction case we need C_abs_intro (which uses semantic typing of body at values).

   For if-terms: is_if t = not abs, so C_intro_notabs applies provided all one-step reducts are C. Good. For app terms: app is not abs; C_intro_notabs applies. So we don't even need C_app_intro separately! C_intro_notabs handles app-shaped terms too. Wait, but C_intro_notabs's proof of the ST_App2 case uses C_app_intro? No, it uses the inductive hypothesis on u. Let's check the proof of C_intro_notabs: it's by induction on T, and the Arrow case does SN-induction on u and uses `C_app_intro`? I wrote "Apply C_app_intro at T2 to term app t u" — that requires C_app_intro. But maybe we can avoid C_app_intro by proving the Arrow closure directly:
       Goal: C T2 (app t u) where T2 = T2a → T2b... no wait, if T = T1→T2, then we need C T2 (app t u). Since T2 is smaller, we can apply the *induction hypothesis of C_intro_notabs at type T2* to the term app t u! Because app t u is not an abstraction, lc, and all its reducts C T2 (as shown). That's exactly `IH_T2 (app t u) (not_abs_app) (lc_app) (reducts)`. 
       
       So C_intro_notabs is self-contained: prove by induction on T; Arrow case:
         forall u C T1 u:
           apply IH(T2) to term app t u:
             - app not abs ✓
             - lc ✓
             - reducts of app t u are C T2:
               + ST_App1 with t --> t0: C T t0 (outer hypothesis) and T = T1→T2, so closure gives C T2 (app t0 u). ✓ (closure of C T t0 is available since C T t0 is the full predicate)
               + ST_App2 with value t, u --> u0: need C T2 (app t u0). Apply the SN-IH on u0 (with C_step Cu), which gives exactly C T2 (app t u0). ✓
               + ST_AppAbs: t abs, contradiction.
         - Wait: SN-induction on u gives IH `forall u', u --> u' -> P u'` where P u' := C T1 u' -> C T2 (app t u'). In the ST_App2 case we have u --> u0 and C T1 u0 from C_step Cu, so P u0 gives C T2 (app t u0). ✓. 
       
       And the outer induction on T is only used to invoke IH at T2 for the app-shaped term. But the app-shaped term's not-abs/lc/reducts don't depend on the IH. Good, no circularity.
       
       Hmm, but there's a subtlety: `C_intro_notabs` recursive call at T2 is legitimate because T2 is a structural subterm of T = T1→T2. ✓.

Excellent! This gives a clean architecture:

Definitions:
```
Fixpoint C (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  lc_at 0 t /\
  strongly_normalizing t /\
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => True
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => forall u, C T1 u -> C T2 (tm_app t u)
  end.
```
Wait, but if T = Arrow T1 T2, C T t requires forall u, C T1 u -> C T2 (app t u). For t = an arbitrary term, fine.

But hold on: `C T2 (tm_app t u)` — for t = abs, C T2 (app abs u) must be proven via C_abs_intro's closure. In C_abs_intro, we prove C (T1→T2) (abs T1 b) which includes closure forall u C T1 u, C T2 (app abs u). The term app abs u is not an abs, lc (needs lc abs and lc u), and its reducts:
   - ST_AppAbs: open b u; needs C T2 (open b u) — from the semantic typing hypothesis H (requires value u).
   - ST_App2: app abs u0 with u --> u0; need C T2 (app abs u0). Apply SN-IH on u0 with C T1 u0 (from C_step). ✓
   - ST_App1: abs --> t0 impossible.
Then C_intro_notabs at type T2 applied to term app abs u gives C T2 (app abs u). ✓.
So C_abs_intro uses C_intro_notabs at type T2 (a subterm of T1→T2, so if C_abs_intro is proven before/independent... C_abs_intro is not by induction on T; it's a general lemma using C_intro_notabs (already proven) and SN induction). Fine.

And C_intro_notabs's Arrow case uses C at T itself in the outer hypothesis `forall t', t --> t' -> C T t'`; no circularity.

Then the fundamental theorem:
```
Theorem fundamental : forall Γ t T, has_type Γ t T ->
  forall σ, env_ok Γ σ -> C T (subst σ t).
```
where env_ok Γ σ := forall x Ty, Γ x = Some Ty -> C Ty (σ x).

Cases:
- T_Var: env_ok applied; subst σ (fvar x) = σ x.
- T_Abs: as designed, using C_abs_intro:
   * lc abs: need lc_at 1 (subst σ t1). Get from typing premise at fresh x: has_type (update Γ x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2, and has_type_lc gives lc (open t1 (fvar x)); then lc_at_1_of_lc_open; then lc_at_subst using env lc.
   * Hsem: forall u, value u -> C T1 u -> C T2 (open (subst σ t1) u).
     Use IH on the premise with the extended environment. Need x fresh from L and fvars t1.
- T_App: IH1 : C (T1→T2) (subst σ t1) = lc(t1') /\ SN /\ forall u, C T1 u -> C T2 (app t1' u). IH2 : C T1 (subst σ t2). Apply closure at u := subst σ t2. Need C T1 (subst σ t2) — that's IH2. So C T2 (app t1' t2'). ✓ 
  This works because closure is over all C-args, not just values. 
- T_True: C Bool true = lc true /\ SN true /\ True.
- T_False: similar.
- T_If: IH1 : C Bool t1' = lc t1' /\ SN t1' /\ True. IH2: C T t2'. IH3: C T t3'. Need C T (if t1' t2' t3').
  Use C_intro_notabs at T applied to tm_if t1' t2' t3':
   - not abs: discriminate.
   - lc: from lc parts.
   - reducts z:
     * ST_IfTrue: z = t2' (up to subst); C T t2' ✓.
     * ST_IfFalse: z = t3'; ✓.
     * ST_If: t1' --> t1'', z = tm_if t1'' t2' t3'. Need C T (tm_if t1'' t2' t3'). Hmm, this is a *new* if-term with a reduced scrutinee. We only know C Bool t1' (lc + SN); from C_step at Bool we get C Bool t1''? C_step requires the C_step lemma at type Bool: C Bool t1' -> t1' --> t1'' -> C Bool t1''. Yes! C Bool includes SN; SN preserved. So C Bool t1''. But C_intro_notabs requires ALL reducts of the new term be C T, and we'd be recursively proving the same statement for a smaller scrutinee. Need SN-induction on t1' to prove: forall t1', C Bool t1' -> C T (if t1' t2' t3'), with t2', t3' fixed C T.
     
     So in the T_If case, prove a lemma `C_if` by SN-induction on the scrutinee:
     ```
     Lemma C_if : forall T b1 b2 b3,
        C Ty_Bool b1 -> C T b2 -> C T b3 -> C T (tm_if b1 b2 b3).
     ```
     Proof: induction on SN b1 (from C Bool b1). Motive P b1 := C Ty_Bool b1 -> C T (tm_if b1 b2 b3).
       intro Cb1. Apply C_intro_notabs at T (term if b1 b2 b3):
        - not abs ✓
        - lc ✓ (Cb1 lc, C b2 lc, C b3 lc)
        - reducts:
          + ST_IfTrue: z = b2, C T b2 ✓ (requires lc b2, lc b3 premises in rule, fine)
          + ST_IfFalse: z = b3 ✓
          + ST_If: b1 --> b1', z = if b1' b2 b3. Need C T (if b1' b2 b3): apply SN-IH at b1' with step b1 --> b1' and C Bool b1' from C_step Cb1 Hstep. ✓
     No induction on T needed; C_intro_notabs already proven. 
     
     Nice.

So the architecture seems solid. Let's now list all required lemmas and think through proofs:

Basic lemmas:
1. `lc_at_mono` maybe: lc_at k t -> k <= k' -> lc_at k' t? Actually we need lc_at k (σ y) given lc_at 0 (σ y), for arbitrary k. Prove `lc_at_weaken : lc_at k t -> k <= k' -> lc_at k' t` by induction on t (or on derivation). Or directly `lc_at_0_any : lc_at 0 t -> forall k, lc_at k t`. Hmm, lc_at 0 means no bvars; then lc_at k for any k: prove mono.

2. `lc_at_subst : forall σ t k, (forall x, lc_at 0 (σ x)) -> (lc_at k (subst σ t) <-> lc_at k t)`. Induction on t; uses lc_at_weaken for the fvar case (need lc_at k (σ x) from lc_at 0). For bvar: subst is same. Good.

3. `lc_at_open : forall k t u, lc_at k u -> lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t)` — needed? For `open (subst σ t1) u` lc maybe. Let's check where we need lc of opened terms:
   - In T_Abs, after getting C T2 (subst σ' (open t1 (fvar x))) from IH, we rewrite to C T2 (open (subst σ t1) u). We don't need an lc lemma, just the equality. 
   - In C_abs_intro's ST_AppAbs case: C T2 (open b u) comes from Hsem. The rewrite in T_Abs gives Hsem's conclusion. Hmm, in the fundamental theorem T_Abs case, C_abs_intro requires Hsem: forall u value, C T1 u -> C T2 (open b u). We derive it by rewriting C T2 (subst σ' (open t1 (fvar x))) to C T2 (open b u) using the equality. So no separate lc open lemma needed.
   - env_ok extension: σ' images: x ↦ u where u value → lc; others σ y lc.
   - For the equality `subst σ' (open t1 (fvar x)) = open (subst σ t1) u`, we need `subst_open` lemma: subst σ (open_rec k u t) = open_rec k (subst σ u) (subst σ t) when σ images are lc? Let's re-derive carefully with k generalization:
     Claim: forall σ t u k, (forall x, lc_at 0 (σ x)) -> subst σ (open_rec k u t) = open_rec k (subst σ u) (subst σ t).
     Proof by induction on t, generalized k.
     - bvar i: LHS = subst σ (if eqb k i then u else bvar i) = if eqb k i then subst σ u else bvar i. RHS = open_rec k (subst σ u) (bvar i) = if eqb k i then subst σ u else bvar i. ✓ (subst on bvar = bvar).
     - fvar x: LHS = subst σ (fvar x) = σ x. RHS = open_rec k (subst σ u) (subst σ (fvar x)) = open_rec k (subst σ u) (σ x). Is σ x locally closed at 0, so open_rec doesn't change it? Need lemma: lc_at 0 v -> forall k u, open_rec k u v = v. Prove `open_rec_lc : forall v, lc_at 0 v -> forall k u, open_rec k u v = v` by induction on v (or on lc derivation). Actually σ x may have bvars? We assume lc_at 0 (σ x), so no bvars, hence open_rec does nothing. ✓.
     - app: IH on both with same k. ✓
     - abs T t: LHS = subst σ (abs T (open_rec (S k) u t)) = abs T (subst σ (open_rec (S k) u t)). RHS = open_rec k (subst σ u) (abs T (subst σ t)) = abs T (open_rec (S k) (subst σ u) (subst σ t)). Apply IH with k := S k. ✓
     - true/false/if: easy.
     Requires σ images lc_at 0. Good.
     
     Then in T_Abs: subst σ' (open t1 (fvar x)) = open_rec 0 (subst σ' (fvar x)) (subst σ' t1) = open_rec 0 u (subst σ' t1). And subst σ' t1 = subst σ t1 because σ' agrees with σ on fvars(t1) (x fresh). Need lemma `subst_agree`:
     `(forall y, In y (fvars t) -> σ y = σ' y) -> subst σ t = subst σ' t`.
     Since σ' = update_env σ x u and x ∉ fvars t, they agree on all y ∈ fvars t. ✓.
     Also need lc_at 0 (σ' y) for all y (for subst_open): for y=x, u is value → lc; else σ y lc from C.

4. `lc_at_1_of_lc_open`: forall t x, lc_at 0 (open t (fvar x)) -> lc_at 1 t. Prove generalized:
   `open_inv_lc : forall t k x, lc_at k (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t) -> lc_at (S k) t`. Induction on t generalized k.
   - bvar i: open_rec k (fvar x) (bvar i) = if eqb k i then fvar x else bvar i.
     If i = k: H : lc_at k (fvar x) (always true); need lc_at (S k) (bvar i) = i < S k. Since i=k, k < S k ✓.
     If i ≠ k: H : lc_at k (bvar i) → i < k → i < S k ✓.
   - fvar: lc_fvar.
   - app: IH both.
   - abs T b: H : lc_at k (abs T (open_rec (S k) (fvar x) b)) → lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) (fvar x) b). Apply IH with k := S k → lc_at (S (S k)) b → lc_at (S k) (abs T b) ✓.
   - if: IH three.
   - true/false ✓.
   Note for abs: lc_at k (abs T body) is by constructor lc_abs: lc_at (S k) body. ✓.

5. `lc_at_subst` then gives lc_at 1 (subst σ t1) ↔ lc_at 1 t1, so lc (abs T1 (subst σ t1)): lc_at 1 (subst σ t1) via lc_at_subst iff lc_at 1 t1. Wait: lc_at_subst requires forall x lc_at 0 (σ x); we have from env_ok → C gives lc_at 0. ✓.

6. `sn_step : SN t -> t --> t' -> SN t'`: induction on SN t or invert: SN_intro H → apply H. 
   ```
   Lemma sn_step : forall t, SN t -> forall t', t --> t' -> SN t'.
   Proof. intros t H; induction H as [t Hall IH]; intros t' Hstep; apply Hall; exact Hstep. Qed.
   ```
   Actually H : SN t is an inductive; destruct it: `inversion H; subst`? Since SN_intro is the only constructor, `destruct H as [t' Hall]`? Hmm, SN_intro : forall t, (forall t', t --> t' -> SN t') -> SN t. So destruct H gives Hall : forall t', t --> t' -> SN t'. Then Hall t' Hstep.
   
7. `sn_multi : SN t -> t -->* t' -> SN t'`: induction on multi derivation. (multi is defined at top.) 
   ```
   Lemma sn_multi : forall t t', t -->* t' -> SN t -> SN t'.
   Proof. intros t t' H; induction H; intros Hsn; auto. apply IHmulti. apply sn_step with (t:=x); auto. Qed.
   ```
   Need to check multi constructors: multi_refl x, multi_step x y z (R x y) (multi R y z).
   
   Do we need sn_multi? Possibly not, but useful.

8. `lc_step : lc t -> t --> t' -> lc t'`: prove by induction on the step derivation; use lc inversion/opening lemmas.
   Cases:
   - ST_AppAbs: t = app (abs T t0) v; t' = open t0 v. Given lc (app (abs T t0) v) → lc (abs T t0) → lc_at 1 t0; lc v. Need lc (open t0 v) = lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 v t0). Lemma `lc_open`: lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t). Prove by induction on t generalized k. Then with k=0. ✓.
   - ST_App1: lc (app t1 t2) → lc t1, lc t2; IH on t1 --> t1' gives lc t1'; lc (app t1' t2) ✓.
   - ST_App2: similar.
   - ST_IfTrue: lc (if true t1 t2) → lc t1 ✓ (result).
   - ST_IfFalse: lc t2.
   - ST_If: lc t1' from IH; lc branches from hyp; construct.
   
9. `lc_open` as above (needed in lc_step and perhaps elsewhere).
   `lc_open_rec : forall t k u, lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t)`.
   Induction on t generalized k:
   - bvar i: open = if eqb k i then u else bvar i. If i=k: u lc_at k ✓. Else bvar i with lc_at k (bvar i) from lc_at (S k) (bvar i) → i < k → i < k ✓.
   - abs: need lc_at k (abs T (open_rec (S k) u t)) ← lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) u t). IH with k+1: lc_at (S (S k)) t → lc_at (S k) u → lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) u t). We have lc_at (S k) u from lc_at k u by weaken. And lc_at (S(S k)) t from H. ✓.
   - others straightforward.
   Requires lc_at_weaken.

11. `C_step : forall T t t', C T t -> t --> t' -> C T t'`. Induction on T.
    - Bool: C t = lc /\ SN /\ True. Need lc t', SN t' from lc_step, sn_step.
    - Arrow T1 T2: C t = lc t /\ SN t /\ (forall u, C T1 u -> C T2 (app t u)). For t': lc t'; SN t'; closure: forall u C T1 u, C T2 (app t' u). We have C T2 (app t u) from closure. And app t u --> app t' u by ST_App1 with premise t --> t' and lc u (from C T1 u). Then IH at T2 (structural subterm) gives C T2 (app t' u). ✓.
    Note: ST_App1 requires locally_closed t2, which is lc u ✓.

14. `has_type_lc : forall Γ t T, has_type Γ t T -> lc t`. Induction on typing derivation.

    ```
    Fixpoint list_max (l : list atom) : atom :=
      match l with [] => 0 | x :: l' => Nat.max x (list_max l') end.
    ```
    Lemma `list_max_spec : forall l x, In x l -> x <= list_max l`.
    Proof induction l; simpl; intros; destruct H; subst; [apply Nat.le_max_l | apply Nat.le_trans with (list_max l); auto; apply Nat.le_max_r].
    Then `fresh_notin : forall l, ~ In (S (list_max l)) l` by intros H; apply list_max_spec in H; lia.

    Actually, to prove open_rec_lc for lc_at 0 only, we can do induction on the term v with the lc hypothesis; but then in the abs case we need IH for body with lc_at 1 body, and our statement is only for lc_at 0. Since under binder, lc_at 1 body may have bvar 0, and open_rec 0 u body would replace it. Wait: if v is lc_at j, then v may have free bvars < j. open_rec k u v replaces bvar k if k < j. So not invariant unless j=0. So induction on the lc_at 0 derivation is the right approach, but the abs constructor gives premise lc_at (S 0) body, whose induction principle... The IH for lc_abs in induction on H : lc_at 0 (tm_abs T body) is for the premise lc_at (S 0) body, which is an lc_at with index 1, not 0. So the induction hypothesis would be `open_rec ...` at index 1? The motive of induction on the derivation H : lc_at 0 v is `fun v H => forall k u, open_rec k u v = v`. When eliminating a derivation with constructor lc_abs, the premise is `lc_at (S 0) body` and the IH is at type `lc_at (S 0) body` — but our motive expects index 0! Since lc_at is an indexed family, `induction H` will generalize the index and produce a motive over both v and the index? Let's see: The standard `induction H` where H : lc_at 0 v will try motive `fun (n:nat) (x:tm) (h:lc_at n x) => ...`? Coq generally requires the conclusion to be generalized appropriately; with H : lc_at 0 v and goal P v, `induction H` gives cases for each constructor with indices unified. In the lc_abs case, index 0 must equal the constructor's k, so k=0, and premise lc_at (S 0) body. The IH is then... Coq gives IH about the premise `lc_at 1 body` but the motive is only defined for the original index 0? The induction principle for lc_at (dependent elimination) is:
    ```
    lc_at_ind : forall P : forall n t, lc_at n t -> Prop,
      (forall k i, i < k -> P k (tm_bvar i) (lc_bvar k i H)) ->
      ... ->
      forall n t (h : lc_at n t), P n t h
    ```
    The premises for lc_abs: forall k T t1 (h : lc_at (S k) t1), P (S k) t1 h -> P k (tm_abs T t1) (lc_abs k T t1 h). So when we instantiate P n t h := (forall k u, open_rec k u t = t), the lc_abs case requires proving `forall k u, open_rec k u (tm_abs T t1) = tm_abs T t1` given IH : forall k u, open_rec k u t1 = t1 — wait, IH is P (S k) t1 h, which with our P (ignoring n) is the same statement. But is it true? P says for t1 with lc_at (S k) t1, open_rec k' u t1 = t1 for all k' u. That's false: t1 = bvar 0 has lc_at 1 but open_rec 0 u (bvar 0) = u ≠ bvar 0. So the induction principle with this P would ask us to prove a false IH? No: the IH is an assumption in the constructor case; we need to derive the conclusion for the abs, using the IH. But the IH as instantiated by the induction principle: P (S k) t1 h = forall k' u, open_rec k' u t1 = t1. This is a false statement in general, but it's an *assumption* in the lc_abs case, and we must prove the lc_abs conclusion from it. But there's a mismatch: our P is not well-typed as a motive for all n because the statement we want is only for n=0. The induction principle requires P : forall n t, lc_at n t -> Prop, so we'd have to define P n t h := (n = 0 -> forall k u, ...). Then in the lc_abs case we get IH : P (S k) t1 h, which is vacuous-ish? P (S k) t1 h = (S k = 0 -> ...) which is vacuous; then we can't prove P k (abs...)     Wait, but we can prove open_rec_lc differently: `lc_at 0 v` means v has no free bvars. We can prove by *term induction* a generalized statement: `forall j, lc_at j v -> forall k, k < j -> forall u, open_rec k u v = v`? Actually if v is lc_at j, then all free bvars are < j, so open_rec k only changes bvar k, and if k >= j, no change. Under binders, opening with open_rec k where k >= j: at depth d, it replaces bvar (k+d) with u, and by lc_at j at depth d we have lc_at (j+d) body, whose free bvars < j+d ≤ k+d, so no replacement.     `forall v j, lc_at j v -> forall k u, j <= k -> open_rec k u v = v.`
    Prove by induction on v, generalizing j k:
    - bvar i: H : i < j, j <= k → i < k → eqb k i = false → open_rec = bvar i. Use Nat.eqb_neq.
    - fvar: refl.
    - app: IH with j, k.
    - abs T b: H : lc_at (S j) b. Goal open_rec k u (abs T b) = abs T b, i.e., open_rec (S k) u b = b. Apply IH_body with j' := S j, k' := S k: need lc_at (S j) b ✓ and S j <= S k ✓.
    - if/true/false: easy.
    Then `open_rec_lc` for lc_at 0: choose j=0, k arbitrary, need 0 <= k ✓.
    
    Great, that's a clean generalized statement. Let me call it `open_rec_lc_gen`.

19. `subst_open_rec` (as in 17). Let me restate:
    ```
    Lemma subst_open_rec : forall σ t u k,
      (forall x, lc (σ x)) ->
      subst σ (open_rec k u t) = open_rec k (subst σ u) (subst σ t).
    ```
    Wait, u here is the term being substituted for bvar; on the RHS it's `subst σ u`. But hold on: in the T_Abs case, u = tm_fvar x, and subst σ' u = σ' x = u_term. Fine.
    Proof by induction on t generalizing k.
    - bvar i: LHS: subst σ (if eqb k i then u else bvar i) = if eqb k i then subst σ u else bvar i. RHS: open_rec k (subst σ u) (subst σ (bvar i)) = open_rec k (subst σ u) (bvar i) = if eqb k i then subst σ u else bvar i. ✓ Simplify with `destruct (Nat.eqb k i); simpl; auto.`
    - fvar x: LHS: subst σ (fvar x) = σ x. RHS: open_rec k (subst σ u) (subst σ (fvar x)) = open_rec k (subst σ u) (σ x) = σ x by open_rec_lc (H x) and k. So need open_rec_lc (σ x) (H x) k (subst σ u) : open_rec k (subst σ u) (σ x) = σ x. Here our open_rec_lc_gen at j=0 works: open_rec_lc_gen (σ x) 0 (H x) k (subst σ u) (Nat.le_0_l k). ✓.
    - app: simpl; rewrite IH1, IH2.
    - abs: simpl; rewrite IH with (k := S k). Note IH is forall k, so use it.
    - if: rewrites.
    - true/false: refl.
    
    One subtlety: `u` is fixed; `subst σ u` appears on the RHS unchanged by k. Fine.

20. In T_Abs, we need `subst σ' t1 = subst σ t1`. We have x ∉ fvars t1 (fresh choice) and σ' = subst_env σ x u. Lemma `subst_agree` with condition: forall y, In y (fvars t1) -> σ y = σ' y. Since x ∉ fvars t1 and σ' y = if eqb x y then u else σ y: for y ∈ fvars t1, y ≠ x, so eqb x y = false, hence σ' y = σ y. Need `Nat.eqb_neq : forall n m, n <> m -> (n =? m) = false`. Then simpl.
    Direction: we want subst σ' t1 = subst σ t1. subst_agree gives subst σ t1 = subst σ' t1 if equality on fvars. Symmetry.

From Stdlib Require Import Lia.

Fixpoint list_max (l : list atom) : atom :=
  match l with
  | [] => 0
  | x :: l' => Nat.max x (list_max l')
  end.

Lemma list_max_spec : forall l x, In x l -> x <= list_max l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| y l IH]; intros x Hin; simpl in *.
  - destruct Hin.
  - destruct Hin as [Heq | Hin].
    + subst; apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (list_max l); [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Definition fresh (l : list atom) : atom := S (list_max l).

Lemma fresh_notin : forall l, ~ In (fresh l) l.
Proof.
  intros l Hin. unfold fresh in Hin.
  apply list_max_spec in Hin. lia.
Qed.
```

`subst`:
```
Fixpoint subst (σ : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => σ x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst σ t1) (subst σ t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst σ t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst σ t1) (subst σ t2) (subst σ t3)
  end.
```

`fvars`:
```
Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => []
  | tm_fvar x => [x]
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvars t1
  | tm_true => []
  | tm_false => []
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2 ++ fvars t3
  end.
```
Note `fvars t1 ++ fvars t2 ++ fvars t3` associates left; fine.

`lc_step`:
```
Lemma lc_step : forall t, lc t -> forall t', t --> t' -> lc t'.
Proof.
  intros t Hlc. induction Hlc as [k i Hlt | k x | k t1 t2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 | k T b H1 IH1 | k | k | k t1 t2 t3 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3].

Hmm, induction on Hlc : lc_at 0 t won't work well for the abs case because we need lc_step for a term under no binder; but induction on the derivation of lc t generalizes k, and t'... The step relation is about t. Maybe better: prove `lc_step` by induction on the step derivation! Since step is an inductive relation, induction on Hstep gives cases for each rule, and we can use the lc hypotheses and opening lemmas. That's cleaner because each step rule gives the structure of t and t'.

```
Lemma lc_step : forall t t', t --> t' -> lc t -> lc t'.

    (* Hlc : lc (app (abs T b) v). Need lc (open b v). *)
    inversion Hlc; subst. (* gives lc (abs T b) and lc v; but lc (abs T b) is lc_at 0 (abs T b) *)
    invert the first to get lc_at 1 b.
    apply lc_open_rec with (k:=0); [..].

Now `env_ok_update`:
```
Definition subst_env (σ : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (u : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else σ y.

Lemma env_ok_update : forall Γ σ x T u,
  env_ok Γ σ -> C T u -> env_ok (update Γ x T) (subst_env σ x u).
Proof.
  intros Γ σ x T u [Hlcall Hdom] Cu. split.
  - intros y. unfold subst_env. destruct (Nat.eqb x y); [apply C_lc with (T:=T); exact Cu | apply Hlcall].

    destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; subst. injection Hty as Heq. subst. exact Cu.
    + apply Hdom. exact Hty.
Qed.
```

Now the fundamental theorem's T_Abs case in detail. Let's write it:

```
  - (* T_Abs *)
    simpl.
    set (x := fresh (L ++ fvars t1)).
    assert (HxL : ~ In x L).
    { intro Hin. apply (fresh_notin (L ++ fvars t1)). apply in_or_app. left. exact Hin. }
    assert (Hty : has_type (update Γ x T1) (open t1 (tm_fvar x)) T2).
    { apply Hbody. exact HxL. }
    assert (Hlcopen : lc (open t1 (tm_fvar x))) by (apply has_type_lc with (Γ := update Γ x T1) (T := T2); exact Hty).
    assert (Hlc1 : lc_at 1 t1).
    { apply (open_inv_lc t1 0 x). change (lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x) t1)). exact Hlcopen. }
    apply C_abs.
    + apply lc_abs. apply (lc_at_subst σ t1 1 (proj1 Hok)). exact Hlc1.
    + intros u Hval Cu.
      pose (σ' := subst_env σ x u).
      assert (Hok' : env_ok (update Γ x T1) σ') by (apply env_ok_update; [exact Hok | exact Cu]).
      specialize (IHbody x HxL σ' Hok').
      assert (Hσ'lc : forall y, lc (σ' y)).
      { intros y. unfold σ', subst_env. destruct (Nat.eqb x y); [apply value_lc; exact Hval | apply (proj1 Hok)]. }
      assert (Hsub : subst σ' (open t1 (tm_fvar x)) = open (subst σ t1) u).
      { unfold open. rewrite (subst_open_rec σ' t1 (tm_fvar x) 0 Hσ'lc).
        (* goal: open_rec 0 (subst σ' (tm_fvar x)) (subst σ' t1) = open_rec 0 u (subst σ t1) *)
        assert (Hx_notin : ~ In x (fvars t1)).
        { intro Hin. apply (fresh_notin (L ++ fvars t1)). apply in_or_app. right. exact Hin. }

Now the final theorem:
```
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  assert (Hok : env_ok empty (fun x => tm_fvar x)).
  { split.
    - intros x; apply lc_fvar.
    - intros x Ty Hx. unfold empty in Hx. discriminate Hx. }
  pose proof (fundamental empty t T Hty (fun x => tm_fvar x) Hok) as HC.
  rewrite subst_id in HC.
  apply (C_sn T t HC).
Qed.
```
`subst_id : forall t, subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t` by induction.
`Hx : empty x = Some Ty` where empty = fun _ => None; `unfold empty in Hx; discriminate Hx`. ✓.

Let's also double-check that `fundamental` yields `C T (subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t)`, then rewrite to C T t. ✓.

For `C_sn`, after destruct T and simpl, `proj1 (proj2 H)` requires the conjunction to associate as `lc t /\ (SN t /\ True)`. Coq parses `A /\ B /\ match...` as `A /\ (B /\ C)` since `/\` is right associative (Infix "/\" := and : type_scope, right associativity). Yes, `and` is right associative. ✓.

Lemma C_abs : forall T1 T2 b,

Lemma C_if : forall T b1 b2 b3,

Qed.

Lemma lc_at_subst : forall σ t k,
  (forall x, lc (σ x)) -> lc_at k t -> lc_at k (subst σ t).

  intros σ t. induction t as [i|x|t1 IH1 t2 IH2|T b IHb| | |t1 IH1 t2 IH2 t3 IH3];

  - inversion Hk; subst. apply lc_true.
  - inversion Hk; subst. apply lc_false.
  - inversion Hk; subst. apply lc_if; [apply IH1 with (k:=k) | apply IH2 with (k:=k) | apply IH3 with (k:=k)]; assumption.
Qed.
```

```coq
(** * Helper definitions and lemmas for strong normalization. *)

From Stdlib Require Import Lia.

(** ** Fresh atoms *)

Fixpoint list_max (l : list atom) : atom :=
  match l with
  | [] => 0
  | x :: l' => Nat.max x (list_max l')
  end.

Lemma list_max_spec : forall (l : list atom) (x : atom),
  In x l -> x <= list_max l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| y l IH]; intros x Hin; simpl in *.
  - destruct Hin.
  - destruct Hin as [Heq | Hin].
    + subst. apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (list_max l); [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Definition fresh (l : list atom) : atom := S (list_max l).

Lemma fresh_notin : forall l : list atom, ~ In (fresh l) l.
Proof.
  intros l Hin. unfold fresh in Hin.
  apply list_max_spec in Hin. lia.
Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma lc_step : forall t t', t --> t' -> lc t -> lc t'.
Proof.
  intros t t' Hstep. induction Hstep as
    [T b v Hlcabs Hvalv
    | t1 t1' t2 Hstep1 IH1 Hlct2
    | v1 t2 t2' Hval1 Hstep2 IH2
    | t1 t2 Hlct1 Hlct2
    | t1 t2 Hlct1 Hlct2
    | t1 t1' t2 t3 Hstep1 IH1 Hlct2 Hlct3];
    intros Hlc.
  - (* ST_AppAbs *) 
    apply lc_at_app_inv in Hlc as [H1 H2].
    apply lc_at_abs_inv in H1.
    apply (lc_open_rec b 0 v H1 H2).
  - apply lc_at_app_inv in Hlc as [H1 H2].
    apply lc_app. { apply IH1. exact H1. } { exact H2. }
  - apply lc_at_app_inv in Hlc as [H1 H2].
    apply lc_app. { exact H1. } { apply IH2. exact H2. }
  - apply lc_at_if_inv in Hlc as [H1 [H2 H3]]. exact H2.
  - apply lc_at_if_inv in Hlc as [H1 [H2 H3]]. exact H3.
  - apply lc_at_if_inv in Hlc as [H1 [H2 H3]].
    apply lc_if. { apply IH1. exact H1. } { exact H2. } { exact H3. }
Qed.
```
Wait, for ST_AppAbs, `lc (open b v)` is `lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 v b)`; `lc_open_rec b 0 v H1 H2` where H1 : lc_at (S 0) b = lc_at 1 b and H2 : lc_at 0 v. The conclusion is lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 v b). ✓.

But careful: in the ST_AppAbs constructor of step, the premises are `locally_closed (tm_abs T t)` and `value v`. The induction on Hstep gives these as hypotheses; the IH for the step? ST_AppAbs has no recursive step premise (Hvalv is not a step), so the pattern is `T b v Hlcabs Hvalv` and no IH. But Coq's induction pattern counts recursive arguments only; Hlcabs : lc_at 0 (tm_abs T b) and Hvalv : value v are not recursive, so pattern names them. However, for constructor arguments that are functions returning Prop? They're not recursive occurrences of step, so no IH. Good.

ST_App1 has a recursive premise `t1 --> t1'` plus `lc t2`; pattern: `t1 t1' t2 Hstep1 IH1 Hlct2`. ✓.

ST_If constructor has recursive premise t1 --> t1', plus lc t2, lc t3: `t1 t1' t2 t3 Hstep1 IH1 Hlct2 Hlct3`. ✓.

Note: The step constructors' argument order: 
```
ST_AppAbs : forall T t v, locally_closed (tm_abs T t) -> value v -> tm_app (tm_abs T t) v --> open t v
ST_App1 : forall t1 t1' t2, t1 --> t1' -> locally_closed t2 -> tm_app t1 t2 --> tm_app t1' t2
ST_App2 : forall v1 t2 t2', value v1 -> t2 --> t2' -> tm_app v1 t2 --> tm_app v1 t2'
ST_IfTrue : forall t1 t2, locally_closed t1 -> locally_closed t2 -> tm_if tm_true t1 t2 --> t1
ST_IfFalse : forall t1 t2, locally_closed t1 -> locally_closed t2 -> tm_if tm_false t1 t2 --> t2
ST_If : forall t1 t1' t2 t3, t1 --> t1' -> locally_closed t2 -> locally_closed t3 -> tm_if t1 t2 t3 --> tm_if t1' t2 t3
```
✓.

However, there's a subtlety: `induction Hstep` will generalize t and t' in the goal. The goal is `lc t -> lc t'`; the induction motive `fun a b H => lc a -> lc b`. This should work.

Now `subst_open_rec`. Let's write:
```coq
Lemma subst_open_rec : forall (σ : atom -> tm) (t u : tm) (k : nat),
  (forall x, lc (σ x)) ->
  subst σ (open_rec k u t) = open_rec k (subst σ u) (subst σ t).
Proof.
  intros σ t. induction t as [i|x|t1 IH1 t2 IH2|T b IHb| | |t1 IH1 t2 IH2 t3 IH3];
    intros u k Hσ; simpl.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k i); reflexivity.
  - rewrite (open_rec_lc_gen (σ x) 0 (Hσ x) k (subst σ u) (Nat.le_0_l k)). reflexivity.
  - rewrite IH1; [rewrite IH2; reflexivity | exact Hσ].
  - rewrite IHb with (k := S k); [reflexivity | exact Hσ].
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IH1; [rewrite IH2; [rewrite IH3; reflexivity | exact Hσ] | exact Hσ].
Qed.
```
Wait, in the bvar case after simpl: goal `(if Nat.eqb k i then subst σ u else tm_bvar i) = (if Nat.eqb k i then subst σ u else tm_bvar i)`? Let's compute: LHS: subst σ (open_rec k u (tm_bvar i)) = subst σ (if eqb k i then u else bvar i) = if eqb k i then subst σ u else bvar i (since subst on bvar = bvar). RHS: open_rec k (subst σ u) (subst σ (tm_bvar i)) = open_rec k (subst σ u) (tm_bvar i) = if eqb k i then subst σ u else bvar i. So after simpl, both sides maybe simplified to the same if. `destruct (Nat.eqb k i); reflexivity` should work if simpl reduced subst on the if. But `simpl` may not push subst into the if because subst is a Fixpoint on its second argument and the second argument is an `if`; simpl might reduce `subst σ (if ...)` by first reducing the if? ```coq
(** * Strong normalization proof. *)

From Stdlib Require Import Lia.

(** ** Fresh atoms *)

Fixpoint list_max (l : list atom) : atom :=
  match l with
  | [] => 0
  | x :: l' => Nat.max x (list_max l')
  end.

Lemma list_max_spec : forall (l : list atom) (x : atom),
  In x l -> x <= list_max l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| y l IH]; intros x Hin; simpl in *.
  - destruct Hin.
  - destruct Hin as [Heq | Hin].
    + subst. apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (list_max l);
        [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Definition fresh (l : list atom) : atom := S (list_max l).

Lemma fresh_notin : forall l : list atom, ~ In (fresh l) l.
Proof.
  intros l Hin. unfold fresh in Hin.
  apply list_max_spec in Hin. lia.
Qed.
```

```coq
(** ** Auxiliary definitions and lemmas for the strong normalization proof. *)

From Stdlib Require Import Lia.

(** *** Fresh atoms *)

Fixpoint list_max (l : list atom) : atom :=
  match l with
  | [] => 0
  | x :: l' => Nat.max x (list_max l')
  end.

Lemma list_max_spec : forall (l : list atom) (x : atom),
  In x l -> x <= list_max l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| y l IH]; intros x Hin; simpl in *.
  - destruct Hin.
  - destruct Hin as [Heq | Hin].
    + subst. apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (list_max l);
        [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Definition fresh (l : list atom) : atom := S (list_max l).

Lemma fresh_notin : forall l : list atom, ~ In (fresh l) l.
Proof.
  intros l Hin. unfold fresh in Hin.
  apply list_max_spec in Hin. lia.
Qed.

(** *** Inversion principles for local closure *)

Lemma lc_at_bvar_inv : forall (k : nat) (i : nat),
  lc_at k (tm_bvar i) -> i < k.
Proof.
  intros k i H. inversion H. assumption.
Qed.

Lemma lc_at_app_inv : forall (k : nat) (t1 t2 : tm),
  lc_at k (tm_app t1 t2) -> lc_at k t1 /\ lc_at k t2.
Proof.
  intros k t1 t2 H. inversion H; subst. split; assumption.
Qed.

Lemma lc_at_abs_inv : forall (k : nat) (T : ty) (b : tm),
  lc_at k (tm_abs T b) -> lc_at (S k) b.
Proof.
  intros k T b H. inversion H; subst. assumption.
Qed.

Lemma lc_at_if_inv : forall (k : nat) (t1 t2 t3 : tm),
  lc_at k (tm_if t1 t2 t3) ->
  lc_at k t1 /\ lc_at k t2 /\ lc_at k t3.
Proof.
  intros k t1 t2 t3 H. inversion H; subst. repeat split; assumption.
Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma lc_at_mono : forall (k : nat) (t : tm),
  lc_at k t -> forall k', k <= k' -> lc_at k' t.
Proof.
  intros k t H. induction H as
    [k i Hlt | k x | k t1 t2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 | k T b H1 IH1 | k | k
    | k t1 t2 t3 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3]; intros k' Hle.
  - apply lc_bvar. lia.
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_app; [apply IH1 | apply IH2]; lia.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IH1. lia.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
  - apply lc_if; [apply IH1 | apply IH2 | apply IH3]; lia.
Qed.
```

Concern: induction on H with k and t variables in context: `intros k t H. induction H`. The goal is `forall k', k <= k' -> lc_at k' t`. The induction motive abstracts k and t. Good. But the `as` pattern: I'm not 100% sure about the number of names. Let's reason: The induction principle for lc_at:
```
lc_at_ind :
forall P : forall (k : nat) (t : tm), lc_at k t -> Prop,
  (forall (k : nat) (i : nat) (l : i < k), P k (tm_bvar i) (lc_bvar k i l)) ->
  (forall (k : nat) (x : atom), P k (tm_fvar x) (lc_fvar k x)) ->
  (forall (k : nat) (t1 t2 : tm) (l : lc_at k t1), P k t1 l -> forall (l0 : lc_at k t2), P k t2 l0 -> P k (tm_app t1 t2) (lc_app k t1 t2 l l0)) ->
  ...
```
When we do `induction H as [ ... ]`, the pattern for lc_app has arguments `k t1 t2` then the two recursive arguments each contribute `Hname IHname`. So `k t1 t2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2`. ✓ as written.
For lc_abs: `forall (k) (T) (t1) (l : lc_at (S k) t1), P (S k) t1 l -> P k (tm_abs T t1) (...)`. Pattern `k T b H1 IH1`. ✓.

But there's a subtlety: The motive P is `fun k t H => forall k', k <= k' -> lc_at k' t`. In the lc_app case, IH1 : P k t1 H1 = forall k', k <= k' -> lc_at k' t1. ✓.

Now, `induction H` when H : lc_at k t and k t are variables: Coq should generalize them. It might complain "Cannot find the elimination combinator" no; lc_at is a straightforward indexed inductive, Scheme lc_at_ind generated. Good.

```coq
Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => []
  | tm_fvar x => [x]
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2
  | tm_abs _ b => fvars b
  | tm_true => []
  | tm_false => []
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fvars t1 ++ (fvars t2 ++ fvars t3)
  end.
```
